**Three nil declarations, stated plainly.** No Main Examination question in any General Studies paper between 2018 and 2026 is owned by this topic; no Essay prompt in that window is owned by it; and no Preliminary General Studies Paper I item before 2026 is owned by it. These are results of a search, not omissions.

**What follows strategically, and it is not what candidates expect.** A nil Mains record does not mean the topic is safe to neglect, for three reasons. **First, the syllabus phrase is explicit**: *art forms, literature and architecture from ancient to modern times*, and cinema is the modern art form in that arc, which makes it a standing Mains possibility under a wording that has not yet been used. **Second, the objective record shows the demand is live and has arrived recently** - the single owned item is from the most recent Preliminary paper held here, and it is built on precisely the distinctions this session trains. **Third, the topic is a natural source of cross-cutting material**: representation, institutional architecture, regulation of expression, preservation and soft power are all live in other papers, and a candidate who holds dated cinema evidence can use it where a question about culture, media or heritage invites an example that nobody else will have. The most probable future framings are the **art-form** family, the **development-and-phases** family, the **certification-against-expression** family, the **institutions-and-preservation** family and the **cinema-and-social-change** family - all five of which are prepared in Lesson 16.

# CUMULATIVE CONCEPT CHECKS

Five blocks, taken at the points where the session's dependencies close. Each is harder than the lesson-local checks because it requires material from several lessons at once.

## Block A - scope and form (Lessons 1-2)

### Concept check

**Question:** A single claim reads: "This award-winning documentary was cleared by the censors and gives an authentic picture of village life in the 1950s." Using the six slots and the four layers of film meaning, identify every distinct error or unsupported step in that sentence.

**Model answer:** **There are five problems - two of slot, two of form, and one of evidence.** **Slot error one - the body.** "The censors" is not a current name. The **Central Board of Film Censors** was set up in **1950** and **renamed the Central Board of Film Certification** under the **Cinematograph Act, 1952**. **Slot error two - the act.** "Cleared" collapses certification into approval. Certification is a **legal precondition for public exhibition** granted on statutory grounds; the Board may certify, **direct modifications and excisions**, or **refuse to sanction**. It expresses no view on merit, so an award and a certificate are unrelated acts - and the sentence's juxtaposition of the two implies a connection that does not exist. **Slot error three, implicit - the award.** "Award-winning" omits the institution, the category and the year, which are the three qualifiers that make an award claim checkable. **Form error - the documentary assumption.** A documentary is a **non-fiction form that still involves selection and framing**; it is not an unmediated record. Its images were arranged or allowed (**mise-en-scene**), framed from a chosen position under chosen light (**cinematography**), selected and ordered so that adjacent shots assert relations (**editing**), and accompanied by chosen speech, ambience or music (**sound**). **Evidence error - "authentic picture".** Because all four layers are choices, the film is **direct evidence of how village life was represented** in that film, and only **indirect** evidence of village life. It is also an **incidental material record** - of streets, objects, dress and speech visible in frame - which is a real but bounded resource. **An accurate version** would name the certificate category granted by the Central Board of Film Certification, name the award's institution, category and year, and describe the film as a documentary account of village life as represented by its makers in the 1950s rather than as an authentic picture of it.

## Block B - the chronology and the plural field (Lessons 3-6)

### Concept check

**Question:** Arrange the following in the order in which each first occurred, and state in one clause what each established: the first indigenously made colour film; the first Indian-shot film; the first cinema house in Madras; the first talking film; the first venture by an Indian; the first indigenous Indian silent feature; the first exhibition of motion pictures in India; the first Indo-British collaboration; the first box-office hit. Then state the structural consequence of the fourth item in your list for the shape of Indian cinema.

**Model answer:** **The order is 1896, 1897, 1899, 1900, 1912, 1913, 1917, 1931, 1937.** **1896 - the first exhibition of motion pictures in India**, the **Lumiere Brothers** showing **six silent films** in Bombay: it established that the medium arrived as an imported novelty, and it is not an Indian film. **1897 - the first Indian-shot film**, *Coconut Fair and Our Indian Empire*: it established that India was being filmed within a year, and says nothing about Indian authorship. **1899 - the first venture by an Indian**, **Harishchandra Bhatavdekar**, known as **Save Dada**, with **two short films** which he also exhibited: it established Indian authorship, in shorts and not in a feature, and shows that making and showing were not yet separate trades. **1900 - the first cinema house in Madras**, by **Major Warwick**: it established permanent exhibition infrastructure, and it belongs with the **Elphinstone Picture House**, Calcutta, **1907**, in showing that exhibition preceded production. **1912 - the first Indo-British collaboration**, ***Pundalik*** by **N.G. Chitre and R.G. Torney**: it establishes why the word *indigenous* is load-bearing in the next entry. **1913 - the first indigenous Indian silent feature**, ***Raja Harishchandra*** by **Dadasaheb Phalke**: it established indigenous feature production, and its mythological subject shows that indigenisation occurred in **story** before technique. **1917 - the first box-office hit**, ***Lanka Dahan***: it established commercial viability, and it is a different film from the 1913 landmark. **1931 - the first talking film**, ***Alam-Ara*** by **Ardeshir Irani**: it established recorded sound, with **W.M. Khan** singing **"De de khuda ke naam par"** **live on set** as the first **recorded** song - **playback**, a song pre-recorded and lip-synced, being a later technique associated with **New Theatres** in **1935**, so that the printed description of Khan as the first playback singer is reported rather than relied on. **1937 - the first indigenously made colour film**, ***Kisan Kanya*** by **Ardeshir Irani**: it established domestic colour **processing** capability, four years after ***Sairandhri*** (**1933**, Prabhat), the first Indian colour film, which was **processed in Germany**. **The structural consequence of the eighth item - the talkie.** A silent film crosses languages by replacing intertitles; a talking film cannot, because dialogue is fixed on the print. Sound therefore **segmented Indian film production by language, permanently**, which explains the timing of Indian cinema's plurality - a plurality that India's linguistic diversity alone cannot explain, since that diversity existed equally in 1913. The consequences are visible in production across **Hindi, Telugu, Tamil, Bhojpuri and other languages**, in the first **south Indian** film appearing in **1939**, eight years after the talkie, and in an administration built for many markets: **nine** regional certification offices, and a **fresh certificate** for every dubbed version.

## Block C - movements, representation and sound (Lessons 7-9)

### Concept check

**Question:** A commentator argues: "Indian cinema's two traditions reflect two attitudes to society - the commercial one escapist, the parallel one honest - and the progress of women's representation from the silent era to today proves that the honest tradition eventually prevailed." Assess every part of this argument.

**Model answer:** **Three claims, and all three fail, though for different reasons.** **On the two traditions.** The sourced distinction is by **purpose**, not by honesty: one form serves primarily as **entertainment**, the other, termed **alternative** or **parallel**, seeks to reflect **the realities of everyday life**. "Escapist" and "honest" import a quality judgement the distinction does not make. More importantly, the difference has an **economic** explanation. From the **studio system** of the mid-1930s, commercial production carried standing costs recoverable only from large audiences, which favours devices that make an audience predictable - genre, stars, song. Parallel cinema, emerging in the **late 1940s** on **poverty, social injustice and human suffering** and drawing on **literature and theatre**, worked at budgets that did not require mass audiences. That is a difference of financing model, not of integrity. **On "eventually prevailed".** The two tracks are contemporaneous and neither displaced the other; the parallel track's reach remained **far narrower**, which is a structural consequence of its financing rather than a verdict on its quality. The two are better described as **mutually enabling**: the commercial track supplied the technical base, exhibition infrastructure and personnel, the alternative track the critical standing. **On the progress of representation.** The sourced periodisation is explicitly **non-linear**. The silent period focused on the **restrictions placed on a woman's life**; **1920-40** directors including **V. Shantaram, Dhiren Ganguli and Baburao Painter** took up **emancipation** themes - the ban on **child marriages**, the abolition of **sati**, then **widow remarriage, women's education and workplace equality**; **1960-80** is described as **extremely stereotypical**, glorifying **motherhood, fidelity and absurd sacrifice**. Representation therefore **moved backwards** across decades in which formal rights expanded, which is the strongest available proof that a film is not a measurement of social conditions but **direct evidence of representation** and of what producers expected audiences to accept. A further complication lies inside the same chronology: the **1935** stunt films, with **Mary Evans** as "**Fearless Nadia**" as the female action lead, fit neither the restriction nor the emancipation band - commercial motive, not reformist intent, produced that agency. **A final caution the argument invites.** The four figures associated with the parallel tradition - **Satyajit Ray, Ritwik Ghatak, Guru Dutt and Shyam Benegal** - are named in the sources as associated with it, and no film title, year or stylistic claim may be attached to any of them without a source.

## Block D - the law, the institutions and the recognitions (Lessons 10-13)

### Concept check

**Question:** A single paragraph reads: "Under the Cinematograph Act, the Censor Board certifies films U, A, UA or S, with appeals to the Film Certification Appellate Tribunal; the National Film Archive of India preserves films, the Directorate of Film Festivals runs IFFI and the National Film Awards, and the 2026 Dadasaheb Phalke Award was given for the best film of the year." Correct every clause, giving the instrument or decision that made each correction necessary.

**Model answer:** **Seven corrections.** **One - the body's name.** It was set up in **1950** as the **Central Board of Film Censors** and **renamed the Central Board of Film Certification** under the **Cinematograph Act, 1952**. **Two - the categories.** **U** and **A** date from the **1952** Act; **UA** and **S** were added by the **1983** upgradation of the **Cinematograph (Certification) Rules**. The **Cinematograph (Amendment) Act, 2023** **retains U, A and S** but replaces the single **UA** with **UA 7+**, **UA 13+** and **UA 16+**, the age endorsement being advisory to **parents or guardians** and **not enforceable by any other person**. **Three - the appeal.** The **Film Certification Appellate Tribunal** was constituted under section **5D**; that section was **omitted by the Tribunals Reforms Act, 2021**, with retrospective effect from **4 April 2021**, and the current route is section **5C**, to the **High Court**. **Four - the archive.** Preservation was housed in the **National Film Archive of India**, **established 1964** at **Pune** under the Ministry of Information and Broadcasting, and now sits within the consolidated **NFDC**, pursuant to the Government of India decision dated **23 December 2020** merging four film media units - the **Films Division**, the **Directorate of Film Festivals**, the **National Film Archive of India** and the **Children's Film Society, India** - with NFDC. **Five - the festival.** **IFFI**, established **1952** and a **competitive** festival, is **organised by NFDC** under the Ministry, with the **Government of Goa** and the **Entertainment Society of Goa**; the Directorate of Film Festivals, set up **1973**, was one of the four merged units. **Six - the awards.** The Directorate's **additional charge** of organising the National Film Awards functions likewise moved with the consolidation; the awards are conferred by the **President of India** under the Ministry's framework, eligibility running to films **certified by the CBFC** within the relevant calendar year. **Seven - the Phalke award.** There is no "2026 award": the ceremony of **22 September 2026** at **Ekta Nagar, Kevadia, Gujarat** conferred the Dadasaheb Phalke Award for **award year 2024**, on **Anant Nag**, the **72nd** edition's winners having been announced on **18 July 2026**; the preceding edition's award, for **award year 2023**, went to **Mohanlal** at a ceremony on **23 September 2025**. And the criterion is not a best film: the award, **instituted 1969**, is for **outstanding contribution to the growth and development of Indian cinema**, selected by a **committee of eminent film-industry personalities**, comprising a **Swarna Kamal** and **ten lakh rupees**.

## Block E - preservation, the digital boundary and construction (Lessons 14-16)

### Concept check

**Question:** A ministry is drafting a ten-year plan for Indian cinema with three objectives: to preserve the national film heritage, to regulate content consistently across theatres, television and streaming, and to raise India's international profile. For each objective, identify the principal structural obstacle and the discipline an answer must observe in discussing it.

**Model answer:** **Objective one - preservation.** The principal obstacle is **selection**, not technology. The technical ladder is known: **preventive conservation** of the storage environment, **conservation** of the carrier against **nitrate and acetate decay** and **colour fading**, **migration** against **format obsolescence**, **restoration** of damaged picture and sound, and **cataloguing and access**. But each is applied per title, and **commercial value rather than cultural value usually decides what is restored**, so preservation inherits the inequality of distribution and a national heritage assembled by default becomes a heritage of whatever was commercially strongest. **The discipline:** state that **digitisation is migration plus access**, not conservation of the carrier, and that it starts a new obsolescence clock; give institutional custody in the two-clause form - housed in the **National Film Archive of India** from **1964**, now within the consolidated **NFDC** after the **23 December 2020** decision; and assert **no holdings, budgets, project names, mission launch year or outlay**, none of which is established. **Objective two - consistent regulation across media.** The obstacle is that the three regimes are **structurally different, not merely differently calibrated**. Cinema has **prior certification** by a statutory Board under the **Cinematograph Act, 1952**, with modification, excision or refusal, and a **cognisable and non-bailable** offence for uncertified exhibition. Television has **no CBFC certificate** for programmes and serials, a **Content/Advertisement Code** under the **Cable Television Network (Regulation) Act, 1995**, and **non-cognizable** offences - though the **2023** amendment requires a **separate certificate** for an **'A' or 'S'** film crossing to television or other prescribed media. Online curated content has **self-classification** under the Code of Ethics in the **IT (Intermediary Guidelines and Digital Media Ethics Code) Rules, 2021**, with **three tiers**, **fifteen-day** grievance timelines and an **inter-departmental committee** under the Ministry. Consistency would require deciding **who classifies** - a Board or the publisher - and **when**, and those are different regulatory designs rather than different settings of one. **The discipline:** assert no rule numbers, platform names or litigation outcomes, and record that the Rules' validity has been challenged in various High Courts without asserting an outcome. **Objective three - international profile.** The obstacle is that recognition is an instrument of **circulation**, not a **measure** of value: an award records one institution, one category, one jury, one year, and the systems - **BAFTA**, the **Academy Awards**, and the prizes of **Cannes, Venice and Berlin** - are separate and non-transferable, with **selection** not even amounting to nomination. **The discipline:** attach institution, category and year to every award claim; never name a recipient without a date; never expand a "first" beyond the category and dated source that supports it; and close on the stronger domestic claim - the **breadth of India's linguistic production** rather than the number of foreign prizes.

# ORIGINAL 10-, 15- AND 20-MARK MAINS MODEL PRACTICE

Six original questions, none of which reproduces or closely paraphrases any verified examination item or any lesson-local question above. Each is followed by a complete model answer within its stated ceiling and a unique quantified scoring note.

## Original 1 - 10 marks, 150 words

**Distinguish between certification, recognition and preservation as acts performed in relation to a film, and explain why the distinction matters for institutional design. (10 marks, 150 words)**

#### Model

The three acts differ in what they change.

**Certification** changes **permissions**. Under the **Cinematograph Act, 1952**, the **Central Board of Film Certification** may certify, **direct modifications and excisions**, or **refuse to sanction**; the certificate is a **legal precondition** for public exhibition, and uncertified exhibition is **cognisable and non-bailable**.

**Recognition** changes **reputation**. The **National Film Awards** and the **Dadasaheb Phalke Award**, **instituted 1969** and selected by a **committee of eminent film-industry personalities**, record a judgement and confer no legal status.

**Preservation** changes **what survives**. It acts on the carrier - against **nitrate and acetate decay**, **colour fading**, **format obsolescence** - over decades.

**Why design follows.** The three have different conflict risks and horizons. A regulator cannot also produce; a jury cannot license; and an archive's decade-long logic sits awkwardly inside a project-organised corporation - the live question raised by the **23 December 2020** consolidation of four film media units into **NFDC**.

**Unique scoring note:** **10 marks = the three acts distinguished by what each changes, in that form 3 + one named instrument or body per act 3 + the different time-horizons and conflict risks identified as the design consequence 2 + the consolidation used as the concrete instance, with its decision date 2.** Treating certification as a mark of quality anywhere in the answer forfeits three marks, because the question is built on exactly that separation.

## Original 2 - 10 marks, 150 words

**"Every award claim in Indian cinema requires an institution, a category and a year." Illustrate with reference to both domestic and international recognitions. (10 marks, 150 words)**

#### Model

Three qualifiers make an award claim checkable.

**Institution.** Bodies are not interchangeable. **BAFTA** is the **British Academy of Film and Television Arts**, the **Academy Awards** the **US Academy of Motion Picture Arts and Sciences**; **Cannes, Venice and Berlin** prizes are awarded **within** festivals. Domestically the **National Film Awards** are conferred by the **President of India** under the Ministry's framework; the **Dadasaheb Phalke Award**, **instituted 1969**, is selected by a **committee of eminent film-industry personalities**.

**Category.** A prize is always a prize *in* something. The **2026** BAFTA result lists ***Boong*** winner in the **Children's and Family Film** category - which "won a BAFTA" cannot support.

**Year.** Award year and ceremony year differ: the Phalke award for **award year 2023** was conferred **23 September 2025**, that for **award year 2024** on **22 September 2026**.

**Without all three a claim is unverifiable** - and a festival **selection** is neither nomination nor win.

**Unique scoring note:** **10 marks = the three qualifiers named as the organising structure 2 + three international systems correctly distinguished as different kinds of body 3 + one exactly categorised instance 2 + the award-year against ceremony-year distinction demonstrated with two dated editions 2 + the selection-nomination-award ladder noted 1.** Naming any award recipient without a date forfeits two marks, since the question is about what makes a claim checkable.

## Original 3 - 15 marks, 250 words

**Assess the proposition that the early history of Indian cinema is better told through its exhibition than through its production. (15 marks, 250 words)**

#### Model

The proposition is right for the first two decades and needs qualification thereafter.

**The exhibition record comes first and is denser.** Motion pictures arrived through an **exhibition** - the **Lumiere Brothers**' **six silent films** in Bombay, **1896**. Permanent venues followed: the first cinema house in **Madras**, **1900**, under **Major Warwick**, and **Jamshedji Madan**'s **Elphinstone Picture House** in **Calcutta**, **1907**. The explanation is causal: the pictures were temporary exhibits and, as the market grew, **created an urgent need for a cinema house**. In Bengal the earliest motion pictures were screened **inside existing theatres** - the **Star**, **Minerva** and **Classic**.

**Production is thinner.** A film **shot** in India in **1897**; **two short films** by **Harishchandra Bhatavdekar**, **1899**; actualities by **F.B. Thanawalla** and **Hiralal Sen**, whose *Indian Life and Scenes* is dated **1903**. The first **indigenous feature** waits until **1913**.

**The analytical gain.** Exhibition explains what production cannot: a paying **audience** was built by importers and venue-owners before an industry existed, making indigenous production commercially conceivable. It also explains **foreign capital** - a Hollywood studio agency, **1916** - and the State's first engagement, which was **fiscal**: entertainment tax in **Calcutta, 1922**, and **Bombay, 1923**.

**The qualification.** After **1918**, with the **Kohinoor Film Company** and Phalke's **Hindustan Cinema Films Company**, production organises into companies and becomes the more informative lens; and the content question - why the first features were **mythologicals** - exhibition cannot answer.

**Verdict:** exhibition is the better lens until roughly 1918 and the indispensable complement thereafter.

**Unique scoring note:** **15 marks = at least four dated exhibition landmarks 4 + the source's own causal statement that temporary exhibits created the need for cinema houses 2 + the production record correctly dated and correctly labelled for contrast 3 + the audience-before-industry inference 3 + the 1918 turn identified as the qualification 2 + a verdict that is periodised rather than absolute 1.** An answer that narrates only film-makers forfeits four marks, because it removes the demand side that the proposition is about.

## Original 4 - 15 marks, 250 words

**"In India's film field, the most important distinction is between what a body does and what it is called." Discuss. (15 marks, 250 words)**

#### Model

The proposition names this field's characteristic error, and the evidence is institutional and statutory alike.

**The regulator's name changed while its job did not.** Set up **1950** as the **Central Board of Film Censors**, it was **renamed** the Central Board of Film **Certification** under the **Cinematograph Act, 1952**. The name says certification; the powers include directing **modifications and excisions** and **refusing to sanction**. The scheme is **certification in form and partly censorship in effect**, and either word alone misdescribes it.

**Four bodies kept their names in print after their functions moved.** By a Government decision dated **23 December 2020**, the **Films Division**, the **Directorate of Film Festivals**, the **National Film Archive of India** and the **Children's Film Society, India** merged with the **National Film Development Corporation**, set up **1975**. Printed sources still attribute festival organisation and the **National Film Awards** functions to the Directorate, and preservation to the archive. Both attributions are correct history and wrong present tense.

**A statutory provision survives in print after its repeal.** Section **5D**, which constituted the **Film Certification Appellate Tribunal**, was **omitted by the Tribunals Reforms Act, 2021**; appeals now lie under section **5C** to the **High Court**.

**And one body's name understates its scope.** A "development corporation" now also archives, produces documentary and children's film, and runs festivals including **IFFI**, established **1952**.

**The practical discipline** is the two-clause sentence: founding fact, then current arrangement. Say **consolidated**, not created.

**Verdict:** names are lagging indicators; function and date are reliable.

**Unique scoring note:** **15 marks = the renaming used as the first and clearest instance, with both operative powers 4 + the consolidation with its exact date and all four merged units 4 + the section 5D omission with its instrument and the current 5C route 3 + the observation that a development corporation now performs four further functions 2 + the two-clause discipline stated as the practical remedy 2.** Writing that NFDC was created by the merger forfeits three marks, because it repeats in reverse the exact confusion the question identifies.

## Original 5 - 20 marks, 250 words

**"Indian cinema is best studied as an archive of social memory rather than as an art form or an industry." Critically examine. (20 marks, 250 words)**

#### Model

The proposition captures something real and, alone, misdescribes all three things it names.

**The archival claim has support.** Films are recorded as reflecting the **socio-economic and political life** of Indians, **shaping national identity** after Independence, reaching **both urban and rural** audiences and creating **empathy with characters**. Because reach does not depend on literacy, cinema is the first Indian form addressing urban and rural publics with one object. Its periodised record of women's representation - **restrictions** in the silent era, **emancipation** themes **1920-40**, an **extremely stereotypical** turn **1960-80**, parallel cinema's **women's real struggles and liberation** - is a usable series.

**But the archive is of representation, not conditions.** Representation regressed while formal rights advanced; they do not move together. A film is direct evidence of how something was **shown** and of what producers expected audiences to accept, and only indirect evidence of the society shown.

**The art-form claim cannot be displaced.** Meaning is produced at four levels - **mise-en-scene, cinematography, editing, sound** - and because these are choices the film is an archive of representation, not a record. Form is the condition of the archive.

**Nor can the industrial claim.** What was represented depended on who financed and distributed: **studio** production from **1935** needed predictable audiences, so a differently financed **parallel** tradition carried subjects the commercial track would not.

**Verdict:** cinema is simultaneously art form, industry, archive of social memory and mass medium; the archival reading holds only because the other two are true, and an answer emphasising one is incomplete.

**Unique scoring note:** **20 marks = the archival case made with at least three sourced claims 4 + the representation-against-conditions correction, with the non-linearity used as its proof 5 + the four layers used to show that form is the condition of the archive 4 + the financing argument used to show that industry determines what is archived 4 + the four-fold characterisation stated in the verdict 2 + an explicit refusal of single-dimension reduction 1.** An answer that accepts the proposition without correcting "social memory" to "representation" forfeits five marks, since that correction is the entire critical content available.

## Original 6 - 20 marks, 250 words

**"A national film policy in India is unavoidably a federal, linguistic and institutional question." Critically examine. (20 marks, 250 words)**

#### Model

The proposition is correct on all three counts, and each has a datable cause.

**Federal.** Certification is a **Union** subject, but the **enforcement of censorship in their respective domains lies with the State governments**, so a certified film meets a second, decentralised layer of control. The division is old: **entertainment tax** came provincially in **Calcutta, 1922** and **Bombay, 1923**, five years before the **Indian Cinematograph Committee** of **1927** raised content centrally. Revenue and content have sat at different levels from the beginning.

**Linguistic.** Recorded sound from **1931** fixed dialogue on the print, so production segmented by language permanently - explaining the **timing** of a plurality India's diversity alone cannot. Administration follows: **nine** regional certification offices under a **Mumbai** head office, and a **fresh certificate** for every **dubbed** version, the same content being a new object in a new language community.

**Institutional.** Six functions were vested in separate bodies - certification (**1950/1952**), training (**1960**, and **1995**), archiving (**1964**), development (**1975**), documentary and children's film, and festivals (**1973**) - and four were merged into **NFDC** by the decision dated **23 December 2020**. Any policy must act through this consolidated architecture, whose archiving horizon is measured in decades.

**The critical qualification.** "Unavoidably" understates the difficulty. Uniform instruments act unevenly across markets of very different scale, and performance-keyed support reproduces inequality rather than correcting it.

**Verdict:** a national film policy is not a single instrument but a negotiation across three axes, and the honest test of any proposal is whether it names all three.

**Unique scoring note:** **20 marks = the federal axis with the certification-enforcement split and at least one dated fiscal anchor 5 + the linguistic axis with sound as the technical cause and two pieces of administrative evidence 5 + the institutional axis with at least five dated bodies and the consolidation date 5 + the qualification that uniform instruments have uneven effects and that performance-keyed support reproduces inequality 3 + a verdict framed as a test any proposal must pass 2.** Explaining the linguistic axis by India's diversity alone, without the 1931 cause, forfeits three marks, because it cannot account for why the segmentation is dated.

# REMEDIATION

Five error sets, each stating the error, why it is made, and the correction that must replace it.

## Error set A - chronology, firsts and credits

| # | The error | Why it happens | The correction |
|---:|---|---|---|
| 1 | Treating 1896 as the date of the first Indian film | the arrival of the medium is remembered as the beginning of the cinema | **1896** is the first **exhibition** - the Lumiere Brothers' **six silent films** in Bombay; the first Indian-shot film is **1897** |
| 2 | Attributing the first Indian venture to Phalke | he is the best-remembered early name | **Harishchandra Bhatavdekar**, known as **Save Dada**, made **two short films** in **1899** and exhibited them |
| 3 | Forgetting that the 1899 work was two **shorts** | "first film by an Indian" sounds like a feature | shorts and features are different landmarks; **1913** remains the first indigenous **feature** |
| 4 | Collapsing *Pundalik* into *Raja Harishchandra* | both are pre-1914 landmarks | ***Pundalik***, **1912**, **N.G. Chitre and R.G. Torney**, is the first **Indo-British collaboration** - which is why *indigenous* is load-bearing in the 1913 claim |
| 5 | Calling *Raja Harishchandra* the first box-office hit | fame and priority are confused | the first hit is ***Lanka Dahan***, **1917**, also by Phalke |
| 6 | Writing that *Kisan Kanya* was the first Indian colour film | the two colour claims look like rival versions of one | ***Sairandhri***, **1933**, Prabhat, is the first Indian colour film, **processed in Germany**; ***Kisan Kanya***, **1937**, Ardeshir Irani, is the first **indigenously made** one |
| 7 | Turning **W.M. Khan** into an actor or music director, or dating **playback** to 1931 | the name is attached to a famous film, and the printed source calls him a playback singer | his recorded credit is **singing** **"De de khuda ke naam par"** in *Alam-Ara*, captured **live on set** - the first **recorded** song; **playback**, a song pre-recorded and lip-synced on set, is associated with **Nitin Bose** at **New Theatres** in **1935**, and the printed wording is reported, not asserted |
| 8 | Saying *Alam-Ara* had the first song in Indian cinema | the qualifier is dropped | **"De de khuda ke naam par"** is the first **recorded** song; live music accompanied films from the 1910s |
| 9 | Misplacing the *Devdas* (1935) claim | the film is famous for other reasons | its recorded first is the **studio system**, an institutional claim |
| 10 | Attaching individual years to *Toofan Mail*, *Punjab Mail* or *Flying Rani* | they appear beside a dated entry | the source **clusters** them with the 1935 stunt entry without separate years |
| 11 | Forgetting *Naujawan*, 1937 | a film defined by an absence is easy to skip | it is recorded as the first film **without any song** - the strongest evidence that song was the default |
| 12 | Extending the *Premsagar* (1939) claim into a language-specific first | "first south Indian film" invites the next step | state it exactly as the source does; do **not** convert it into a first in any named language, and note that Madras had a cinema house from **1900** |
| 13 | Stating Fatma Begum's achievement as acting or as post-sound | her credit is usually simplified | **1926**, silent era, she **produced and directed** her own film, ***Bulbul-e-Paristan*** |
| 14 | Reversing the entertainment-tax cities | the two years run together | **Calcutta 1922**, **Bombay 1923** |
| 15 | Dating the two film companies to any year but 1918 | the decade blurs | **1918** - the **Kohinoor Film Company** and Phalke's **Hindustan Cinema Films Company** |

## Error set B - plurality, representation and the use of film as evidence

| # | The error | Why it happens | The correction |
|---:|---|---|---|
| 16 | Using *Indian cinema* to mean Hindi cinema | the dominant distribution centre stands for the whole | Indian cinema is **multilingual by structure**: production across **Hindi, Telugu, Tamil, Bhojpuri and others**, nine regional certification offices, a **fresh certificate** for every dubbed version |
| 17 | Explaining plurality by India's diversity alone | it is the available generalisation | that cannot explain the **timing**; segmentation dates from **recorded sound in 1931**, because a talking film cannot swap its language as intertitles could |
| 18 | Treating the source's four named languages as the complete set | a list looks exhaustive | the source writes "**etc.**"; the list is illustrative |
| 19 | Converting scale claims into figures | an answer wants a number | **no output, screen, revenue or ranking figure** is established here; use the qualitative characterisation |
| 20 | Attaching a year or a cap to the foreign-investment provision | "in recent years" invites a date | the source supplies **no year**; none is asserted |
| 21 | Writing that films "mirror society" | it is the default sentence | a film is **direct** evidence of **representation** and of what producers expected audiences to accept; **indirect** evidence of conditions |
| 22 | Reading the women's periodisation as a progress narrative | periodisations look linear | it is explicitly **non-linear**: reform themes in **1920-40**, **extremely stereotypical** in **1960-80**, while formal rights advanced |
| 23 | Filling the 1940-60 gap in the periodisation | the sequence looks incomplete | the source characterises no period between 1940 and 1960; the gap is recorded, not filled |
| 24 | Ignoring the 1935 stunt-film counter-case | it does not fit the periodisation | **Mary Evans**, "**Fearless Nadia**", was the female action lead of the mid-1930s stunt film - commercial motive, not reformist intent, produced that agency |
| 25 | Attributing films or styles to Ray, Ghatak, Guru Dutt or Benegal | the names are famous enough to feel like knowledge | the sources name them **as associated with the parallel tradition** and supply **no titles, years or stylistic claims** |
| 26 | Dating parallel cinema to the 1970s or to a state institution | institutional finance is assumed to be the cause | it emerged in the **late 1940s**, nearly three decades before the development corporation of **1975** |

## Error set C - the law, and the statements that have expired

| # | The error | Why it happens | The correction |
|---:|---|---|---|
| 27 | Naming the Film Certification Appellate Tribunal as the current appeal forum | it is printed in circulating material | section **5D** was **omitted by the Tribunals Reforms Act, 2021**, retrospective from **4 April 2021**; appeals lie under section **5C** to the **High Court** |
| 28 | Attributing the tribunal's abolition to the film statute | the change is assumed to be film policy | it came through a **general tribunals-reform statute** |
| 29 | Giving the old UA description as current | it was the position from 1983 | **UA 7+**, **UA 13+** and **UA 16+** replaced it under the **2023** amendment; **U, A and S are retained** |
| 30 | Treating the age markers as enforceable restrictions | a category looks like a bar | the endorsement **informs the guidance of parents or guardians** and is **not enforceable by any other person** |
| 31 | Saying certificates lapse after ten years | that was the earlier rule | certificates are now **perpetually valid** |
| 32 | Dating the Board's founding to 1952 | the Act is remembered, the body is not | the Board was set up in **1950** as the **Central Board of Film Censors** and **renamed** under the 1952 Act |
| 33 | Stating a fixed number of Board members | ranges are hard to hold | a **Chairman** plus **not less than 12 and not more than 25** members |
| 34 | Naming only two outcomes of Board examination | the extremes are memorable | three: certify; **direct modifications and excisions**; **refuse to sanction** |
| 35 | Reversing the federal split | both halves are central-sounding | **certification is a Union subject**; **enforcement of censorship** lies with the **State governments** |
| 36 | Reversing the offence asymmetry | two statutes, two characterisations | exhibiting an **uncertified film** is **cognisable and non-bailable**; offences under the **cable** statute are **non-cognizable** |
| 37 | Extending the Doordarshan exception to television generally | one exception is read as a rule | television programmes and serials are covered by a **different statute**, not by that exception |
| 38 | Saying streaming is unregulated | the CBFC is absent, so regulation seems absent | online curated content falls under the **IT (Intermediary Guidelines and Digital Media Ethics Code) Rules, 2021**, with **self-classification**, a **Code of Ethics** and **three tiers** |

## Error set D - institutions, names and the merger

| # | The error | Why it happens | The correction |
|---:|---|---|---|
| 39 | Naming NFDC among the four merged units | the merger involves five names | the four are **Films Division**, **Directorate of Film Festivals**, **National Film Archive of India** and **Children's Film Society, India**; **NFDC** is the receiving body |
| 40 | Saying NFDC was created by the merger | absorption looks like creation | it was set up in **1975** and had already absorbed the **Film Finance Corporation** and the **Indian Motion Picture Export Corporation** in **1980**; say **consolidated**, not created |
| 41 | Including the regulator or a training institute in the merger | all are ministry bodies | the **CBFC**, **FTII** and **SRFTI** were **not** merged - which is itself evidence that the functional distinctions are real |
| 42 | Saying the Directorate of Film Festivals organises IFFI today | it is printed that way | **IFFI**, established **1952**, is organised by **NFDC** under MIB with the **Government of Goa** and the **Entertainment Society of Goa** |
| 43 | Writing that FTII was founded under that name | the acronym is familiar | set up in **1960** as the **Film Institute of India**; **Television Wing 1971**, re-designated; **registered as a Society in October 1974** under the **Societies Registration Act, 1860** |
| 44 | Confusing the two institutes' registration statutes | both are societies | Pune under the **central** Societies Registration Act, **1860**; Kolkata under the **West Bengal** Societies Registration Act, **1961** |
| 45 | Omitting the archive's founding year or location | it is absorbed into the merger story | **National Film Archive of India**, **established 1964**, under MIB, at **Pune** |
| 46 | Asserting archive holdings, budgets, project names or a mission launch year | an answer wants a number | **none is established here**; the film heritage mission is taught by **function only** |
| 47 | Asserting an effect of the consolidation | the evaluative question invites a verdict | report the **rationale** and state the **risk** - differing horizons and constituencies - without asserting an outcome |
| 48 | Listing acronyms instead of functions | the bodies are remembered as names | organise by the **six jobs**: certify, train, archive, develop, produce documentary and children's film, exhibit and reward |

## Error set E - awards, status and construction discipline

| # | The error | Why it happens | The correction |
|---:|---|---|---|
| 49 | Writing "won an international award" | the qualifiers feel pedantic | an award claim requires the **institution**, the **category** and the **year** |
| 50 | Treating BAFTA and the Academy Awards as one body | both are foreign film honours | **BAFTA** is the **British Academy of Film and Television Arts**; the **Academy Awards** are the **US Academy of Motion Picture Arts and Sciences**; **Cannes, Venice, Berlin** prizes are **festival** prizes |
| 51 | Converting a festival selection into a prize | the retelling inflates | **selection** is programming; **nomination** is a shortlist in a category; **award** is a win. Three distinct statuses |
| 52 | Equating award year with ceremony year | one date is remembered | the Dadasaheb Phalke Award for **award year 2023** was conferred on **23 September 2025**; that for **award year 2024** on **22 September 2026** |
| 53 | Naming an award recipient without a date | recipients feel like stable facts | every recipient is a photograph with a date; the canonical record verified in early September 2026 named the **2023** recipient, and the **2024** award was conferred three weeks later |
| 54 | Describing the Dadasaheb Phalke Award as a National Film Award category, or as an award for a film | both are national honours | it is a separate honour for a **person**, for **outstanding contribution to the growth and development of Indian cinema** |
| 55 | Omitting the certification eligibility gate for the national awards | regulation and recognition are filed separately | eligibility runs to films **certified by the CBFC** within the relevant calendar year - sequence, not identity |
| 56 | Expanding a "first Indian film" claim | the milestone is the exciting part | tie it to the **exact category** and a **dated source**; do not widen it to any recognition from that body |
| 57 | Opening an answer with a definition | it feels safe | open with a **qualified thesis**; seven are available |
| 58 | Writing a 20-mark answer as a longer 10-mark answer | more marks suggest more examples | extra marks are paid for in **dimensions** and **verdict precision** |
| 59 | Closing with a transplantable verdict | conclusions are written last and fastest | the verdict must answer the **exact wording** and be unusable in another answer |
| 60 | Using either prohibited narrative | they are the two available scripts | replace the triumph story with the **structural** claim, and the censorship lament with the **scheme-and-powers** analysis |

## Drill sets

**Drill A - name the qualifier.** For each claim, state the exact qualifying word that makes it true and the film it belongs to: the first colour film; the first indigenously made colour film; the first talking film; the first recorded song; the first film without any song; the first indigenous silent feature; the first box-office hit. *(Expected: the qualifier is the discriminating word in every case - Indian colour, indigenously made, talking, recorded, without any song, indigenous silent feature, box-office hit.)*

**Drill B - date or refuse.** Which of these may be written without a verification date: the Cinematograph Act's year; India's annual film output; the CBFC's founding year; the number of Indian screens; the archive's founding year; the current Dadasaheb Phalke recipient; the year the four film media units were decided to be merged; the film heritage mission's launch year. *(Expected: statute, institution and decision years may; outputs, screen counts, current recipients and unresolved launch years may not.)*

**Drill C - which regime.** For each, name the instrument: a feature released in theatres; a serial broadcast on cable television; a film made especially for the official broadcaster; an 'A'-certified film shown on television; a curated on-demand online catalogue.

**Drill D - which status.** For each phrase, say whether it names selection, nomination, an award, or a certificate: programmed at a festival; shortlisted in the documentary category; granted UA 13+; winner in the Children's and Family Film category; refused sanction for public exhibition.

**Drill E - route the function.** Send each to the right body and state its founding year: certifying a film for public exhibition; training a cinematographer in Pune; training a sound designer in Kolkata; preserving a nitrate print; financing a film the market will not; organising India's international film festival; organising the national film awards functions.

**Drill F - founding or current.** For each statement, say whether it is a founding fact, a current arrangement, or both: the archive was established in 1964 at Pune; the Directorate of Film Festivals organises IFFI; NFDC's functions include preservation; the Board is called the Central Board of Film Censors; appeals lie to the High Court; certificates are valid for ten years.

## Answer spines

**Spine 1 - art-form demand (10 or 15 marks).** composite-form thesis -> the five combined inputs -> reproducibility and mass simultaneity as the two additions -> the regulatory consequence -> one dated Indian anchor -> the limit, that popularity is not the credential -> verdict placing cinema in the ancient-to-modern arc.

**Spine 2 - development and phases (15 marks).** indigenisation thesis -> exhibition 1896 and the venues -> Indian authorship 1899 -> indigenous feature 1913 with mythological content -> sound 1931 and linguistic segmentation -> colour 1933 and 1937 with the processing distinction -> studio 1935 -> parallel from the late 1940s -> verdict on speed of appropriation with the competitive-market qualification.

**Spine 3 - certification and expression (15 marks).** certification-in-form thesis -> statute 1952 and the Board's founding, renaming and composition -> the four grounds split into community-offence and audience-protection -> the three outcomes with modification and excision as decisive -> the federal split -> the current position after 2021 and 2023 -> verdict distinguishing classification from content control, with the polity boundary named.

**Spine 4 - institutional architecture (15 marks).** six-jobs thesis -> bodies with founding years -> the 23 December 2020 consolidation of four units into NFDC -> the exclusion of the regulator and the training institutes -> the rationale and the horizon risk -> verdict that the test is whether specialist mandates survive.

**Spine 5 - recognition and soft power (15 or 20 marks).** instrument-not-measure thesis -> the three mechanisms by which awards matter -> the four qualifiers that bound what an award establishes -> the separate systems -> one exactly categorised dated instance -> award year against ceremony year -> verdict on domestic breadth against foreign prizes.

**Spine 6 - preservation and the digital boundary (15 or 20 marks).** selection-before-technology thesis -> the decay ladder and its matching interventions -> digitisation as migration plus access -> institutional custody in two clauses -> commercial rather than cultural value deciding what is restored -> the inheritance of distributional inequality -> verdict on public custody as a corrective.

       The source's "first playback singer" wording for Khan is

        |    separates VOICE from BODY; song becomes a commodity
        +--> LANGUAGE SEGMENTATION: a talking film cannot swap

  1933 Sairandhri (Prabhat) - first Indian colour, PROCESSED IN GERMANY

 ====================== TWO TRACKS ====================================
  ENTERTAINMENT CINEMA          |  PARALLEL / ALTERNATIVE (late 1940s)
  studio overheads -> volume    |  poverty, social injustice,

                                |  far narrower reach - STRUCTURAL

  silent: restrictions on a woman's life
  1920-40: emancipation themes (child marriage ban, sati abolition,

           - V. Shantaram | Dhiren Ganguli | Baburao Painter
  1960-80: EXTREMELY STEREOTYPICAL (motherhood, fidelity, sacrifice)

  -> 1995 CABLE TV ACT (separate regime, NON-COGNIZABLE offences)
  -> 2021 TRIBUNALS REFORMS ACT (s.5D FCAT OMITTED, retro 4 Apr 2021;

  SELECTION   -> programmed to screen
  NOMINATION  -> shortlisted IN A CATEGORY

     cinema; industry committee selects; Swarna Kamal + Rs 10 lakh;

  AWARD YEAR =/= CEREMONY YEAR:  2023 -> conferred 23 Sep 2025
                                 2024 -> conferred 22 Sep 2026

     | Cannes/Venice/Berlin (festival prizes) - three kinds of body
  Boong: 2026 BAFTA, CHILDREN'S AND FAMILY FILM category;
     directed by Lakshmipriya Devi
                                  |
 ====================== PRESERVATION AND THE DIGITAL EDGE =============
  DECAY: nitrate and acetate | colour fading | FORMAT OBSOLESCENCE
  ANSWERS: preventive conservation -> carrier conservation ->
           migration -> restoration -> cataloguing and access
  DIGITISATION = migration + access, NOT carrier conservation
  RUNG 0: SELECTION - commercial rather than cultural value usually
          decides what is restored -> preservation inherits the
          inequality of distribution
  THREE REGIMES FOR MOVING IMAGES:
     cinema -> prior certification by a statutory Board
     television -> a content code under a broadcasting statute
     online curated content -> SELF-CLASSIFICATION under the
        IT Rules, 2021, three tiers, 15-day grievance timelines,
        inter-departmental committee under MIB
                                  |
                                  v
   FORM -> PHASE -> PLURALITY -> MOVEMENT -> REPRESENTATION ->
   REGULATION -> INSTITUTION -> RECOGNITION -> PRESERVATION -> ANSWER

## Map 2 - decision tree: identifying any cinema claim in six questions

```text
                    A CLAIM ARRIVES
                          |
          Q1  WHICH FILM? title + language + year
              |                        |
         all three?               any missing? -> the claim is not
              |                                   yet checkable
              v
          Q2  WHOSE CREDIT, EXACTLY?
              produced | directed | acted | sang playback |
              shot | exhibited
              -> a credit is not transferable between people
                          |
          Q3  WHICH CLOCK?
              shooting | exhibition | production | certification |
              award year | ceremony year | institutional founding
              -> if the sentence has one date and needs two, stop
                          |
          Q4  WHICH INSTRUMENT OR BODY?
              Act? Rules? Board? corporation? archive? festival?
              training institute?  -> and does it still exist
                                      under that name?
                          |
          Q5  WHICH AWARD, IN WHICH CATEGORY?
              institution + category + year, or the claim fails
                          |
          Q6  WHAT IS THE STATUS?
              selected | nominated | awarded
              certified | modified | refused
              existing | renamed | merged | omitted
                          |
                          v
              IF ALL SIX ARE ANSWERABLE -> write the sentence
              IF ANY IS NOT -> write the narrower sentence that is
```

## Map 4 - three regimes for moving images, side by side

| | **Cinema** | **Television** | **Online curated content** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Instrument | Cinematograph Act, **1952** | Cable Television Network (Regulation) Act, **1995** | IT (Intermediary Guidelines and Digital Media Ethics Code) Rules, **2021**, notified **25 February 2021** |
| Who classifies | the **Central Board of Film Certification** | - | the **publisher**, by self-classification |
| Timing | **before** exhibition, work by work | a code applied to output | classification before publication; State involvement afterwards |
| Categories | U, A, S, and **UA 7+ / 13+ / 16+** | - | age-appropriate categories with **age verification** |
| Characteristic remedy | certify, **modify and excise**, or **refuse to sanction** | code compliance | complaint, escalation, advisory, order, emergency blocking |
| Oversight structure | statutory Board under MIB; appeal under s. **5C** to the **High Court** | the content code framework | **three tiers**: publisher, publishers' association, central government; **15-day** timelines; **inter-departmental committee** under MIB |
| Offence characterisation | uncertified exhibition: **cognisable and non-bailable** | **non-cognizable** | not a certification offence |
| Bridges and exceptions | films made especially for the official broadcaster are the stated exception | TV programmes and serials need **no CBFC certificate** | - |
| Cross-media bridge, **2023** | an **'A' or 'S'** film needs a **separate certificate** for television or other prescribed media | | |
